Remark 2.2
.

Notice that the hypothesis that all the 2-cells of
\mathpzc{K}
are isomorphic is needed to define both the action
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
on morphisms and 2-cells. Indeed, in order to use the universal property of iso-comma objects to induce the desired morphisms we need to start from isomorphic 2-cells.

We now aim at proving that the quotient pre-2-stacks of Definition
2.1
are well-defined and that they are trihomomorphisms from
\mathpzc{K}^{\operatorname{op}}
into
2\mathpzc{Cat}
. We will split the proof in various intermediate results.

Proposition 2.3
.

Let
\mathcal{Y}\in\mathpzc{K}
. Then
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
(defined in
2.1
) is a 2-category.

Proof.

Straightforward using the fact that
2\mathpzc{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{Y})
is a 2-category and the additional data given by the morphisms into the fixed object
\mathcal{X}
behave well with respect to identity and composition.
∎

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
f:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a morphism in
\mathpzc{K}
. Then
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)
(defined in
2.1
) is a 2-functor.

Proof.

The fact that
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)
is well-defined follows by the closure of principal-
\mathcal{G}
-2-bundles under iso-comma-objects (Proposition
1.19
). We now prove the 2-functoriality of
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)
.

Firstly, we observe that, using the two-dimensional universal property of the iso-comma object, it is straightforward to prove that the assignment on 2-cells preserves identities and composition.

Let now
(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})
be an object of
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
. Since both
\operatorname{id}_{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}}\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057pt\pi_{\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}f)}
and
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\operatorname{id}_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})})
can be induced by the universal property of
\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}
using the same 2-cell, they are equal. So
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)
preserves identities.

Let now
\rho\colon(\mathcal{R},\alpha_{\mathcal{R}})\to(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})
and
\varphi\colon(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})\to(\mathcal{Q},\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}})
be morphisms in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
. Since both
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\varphi)\circ[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\rho)
and
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\varphi\circ\rho)
can be induced by the universal property of
\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}
using the same 2-cell, they are equal. So
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)
preserves composition.
∎

Proposition 2.5
.

Let
\Lambda\colon f\Rightarrow g
be a 2-cell in
\mathpzc{K}
from
f\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
to
g\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}.
Then
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda)
(defined in
2.1
) is a 2-natural transformation.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\colon(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})\to(\mathcal{Q},\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}})
be a morphism in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
. We need to prove that the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda)_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda)_{(\mathcal{Q},\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}})}}

Thanks to the universal property of the iso-comma object
\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}
, this is implied by the fact that the following diagrams are commutative by construction:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda)_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\ast}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\ast}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\ast}g}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda)_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}

∎

Remark 2.6
.

The previous three propositions (
2.3
,
2.4
and
2.5
) show that
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
takes values in
2\mathpzc{Cat}
.

Before proving that
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
is a trihomomorphism, we recall the definition of trihomomorphism. We take
[
8
]
as main reference.

Definition 2.7
.

Let
T
and
T^{\prime}
be tricategories. A
trihomomorphism

F\colon T\to T^{\prime}
is given by the following data:

•

a function
F:\operatorname{Ob}(T)\to\operatorname{Ob}(T^{\prime})
;

•

given
a,b\in T
a pseudofunctor

F_{a,b}\colon T(a,b)\to T^{\prime}(F(a),F(b));

•

given
a,b,c,d\in T
an adjoint equivalence
\chi\colon\otimes^{\prime}\circ(F\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptF)\Rightarrow F\circ\otimes
with left adjoint

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T(b,c)\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT(a,b)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(b),F(c))\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(F(a),F(b))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T(a,c)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(a),F(c));}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptF}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

•

given
a\in T
an adjoint equivalence
\iota\colon I^{\prime}_{F(a)}\Rightarrow F\circ I_{a}
with left adjoint

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T(a,a)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(a),F(a));}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\prime}_{F(a)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

•

given
a,b,c,d\in T
an invertible modification

\hbox to199.32pt{\vbox to78.99pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 99.65787pt\lower-40.64609pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Definition 2.8
.

Let
F,G\colon T\to T^{\prime}
be trihomomorphisms. A
tritransformation

\theta\colon F\Rightarrow G
is given by the following data:

•

given
a\in T
a morphism
\theta_{a}\colon F(a)\to G(a)
in
T^{\prime}
;

•

given
a,b\in T
an adjoint equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T(a,b)}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(a),F(b))}{}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(G(a),G(b))}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(a),G(b));}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}(1,\theta_{b})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}(\theta_{a},1)}

•

given
a,b,c\in T
invertible modifications

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T(b,c)\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT(a,b)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(b),F(c))\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(F(a),F(b))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(G(b),G(c))\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(F(a),F(b))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(b),G(c))\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(F(a),F(b))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T(a,c)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(G(b),G(c))\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(G(a),G(b))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(G(b),G(c))\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(F(a),G(b))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(a),G(c))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(G(a),G(c))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptG}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptF}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptF}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057pt1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}(1,\theta_{c})\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057pt1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057pt\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}(\theta_{b})\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057pt1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(1,\theta_{b})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(\theta_{a},1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}(\theta_{a},1)}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T(b,c)\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT(a,b)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(b),F(c))\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(F(a),F(b))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(b),F(c))\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(F(a),F(b))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(b),G(c))\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptT^{\prime}(F(a),F(b))}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(a),F(c))}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(G(a),G(c))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptF}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057ptF}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}(1,\theta_{c})\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057pt1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}(1,\theta_{c})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}(\theta_{a},1)}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T(a,a)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F(a),F(a))}}

Proposition 2.9
.

The assignment
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
of Definition
2.1
is a trihomomorphism.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z}
in
\mathpzc{K}
. We prove that the assignment

[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z}}\colon\mathpzc{K}^{\operatorname{op}}(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z})\to 2\mathpzc{Cat}([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y}),[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Z}))

that sends a morphism
f\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
into
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)
and a 2-cell
\Gamma\colon f\Rightarrow g
into
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Gamma)
is a 2-functor.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a morphism in
\mathpzc{K}
. The 2-natural transformation
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\operatorname{id}_{f})
has component relative to
(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})\in[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
induced by the universal property of the iso-comma object
\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}
as in the following diagram

and so
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\operatorname{id}_{f})_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}
is equal to
(\operatorname{id}_{[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)})_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}
. This shows that
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z}}
preserves identities.

Let now
f,g,h\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
be morphisms in
\mathpzc{K}
and
\Gamma\colon f\Rightarrow g
and
\Lambda\colon g\Rightarrow h
be 2-cells in
\mathpzc{K}
. The 2-natural transformation
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda\circ\Gamma)
has component relative to
(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})\in[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
induced by the universal property of the iso-comma object
\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{h}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}
as in the following diagram

and so
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda\circ\Gamma)_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}
is equal to
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda)_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}\circ[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Gamma)_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}
. This shows that
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z}}
preserves composition.

Let now
\mathcal{Y}\in\mathpzc{K}
. We need to prove that there exists an adjoint equivalence

\iota\colon\operatorname{id}_{[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})}\Rightarrow[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}})

Given
(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})\in[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
, we define

\iota_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}\colon\operatorname{id}_{[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})}((\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}}))\to[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}})((\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}}))

as an adjoint equivalence between
(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})
and
(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Y},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\pi_{\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}})
given by the fact that they are both bi-iso-comma objects of
\pi_{\mathcal{P}}
and
\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}
.
Then, given a morphism
\varphi\colon(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})\to(\mathcal{Q}\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}})
in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
, we can induce the invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Y}}{}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}})(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{(\mathcal{Q},\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}})}}

using the universal property of the iso-comma object
\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Y}
thanks to the following compatible isomorphic 2-cells:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}})(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\ast}\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}})(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\ast}\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{P}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\varphi}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}

Moreover, it is straightforward to prove that the assignment of the structure 2-cells
\iota_{\varphi}
is pseudofunctorial and that
\iota
is an adjoint equivalence.

Let now
\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T}\in\mathpzc{K}
. We need to prove that there exists an adjoint equivalence

\chi\colon\otimes^{\prime}\circ([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057pt[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}])\Rightarrow[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]\circ\otimes

Given
g\colon\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{Z}
and
f\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
, we need to define a 2-natural transformation

\chi_{f,g}\colon[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)\circ[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)\Rightarrow[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f\circ g)

Given
(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})\in[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
, we define

(\chi_{f,g})_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}\colon([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)\circ[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f))((\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}}))\Rightarrow[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f\circ g)((\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}}))

as an adjoint equivalence between
(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}
given by the fact that they are both iso-comma objects of
\pi_{\mathcal{P}}
and
f\circ g
.

Given a morphism
\varphi\colon(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})\to(\mathcal{Q}\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}})
in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
, the following square is then commutative

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi_{f,g})_{(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)\circ[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f))(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f\circ g)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi_{f,g})_{(\mathcal{Q},\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}})}}

thanks to the following commutative diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}})^{\ast}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)\circ[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g))(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi_{f,g})_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}(f\circ g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f\circ g)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\ast}(f\circ g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}})^{\ast}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi_{f,g})_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\ast}(f\circ g)}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}(f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)\circ[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g))(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi_{f,g})_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f\circ g)^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f\circ g)(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f\circ g)^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}(f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi_{f,g})_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f\circ g)^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}

This shows that
\chi_{f,g}
is 2-natural. Moreover, when
f
and
g
vary the
\chi_{f,g}
’s form a 2-natural transformation. Indeed, given morphisms
g,g^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{Z}
and
f,f^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
and 2-cells
\Gamma\colon g\Rightarrow g^{\prime}
and
\Lambda\colon f\Rightarrow f^{\prime}
the following equality holds

[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda\star\Gamma)\circ\chi_{f,g}=\chi_{f^{\prime},g^{\prime}}\circ([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda)\star[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Gamma).

This is because, for every
(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})\in[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
, the following diagram is commutative

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f\circ g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}\circ g^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi_{f,g})_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)(([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda))_{\mathcal{P}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda\star\Gamma))_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Gamma))_{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{f^{\prime},g^{\prime}}}

thanks to the commutativity of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f\circ g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}\circ g^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}\circ g^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\ast}\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\ast}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi_{f,g})_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)(([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda))_{\mathcal{P}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}(f\circ g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda\star\Gamma))_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}(f^{\prime}\circ g^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((f^{\prime})^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}})^{\ast}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Gamma))_{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda)_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((f^{\prime})^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}})^{\ast}g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{f^{\prime},g^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}(f^{\prime}\circ g^{\prime})}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f\circ g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}\circ g^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}\circ g^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}(f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi_{f,g})_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](g)(([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda))_{\mathcal{P}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f\circ g)^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Lambda\star\Gamma))_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}(f^{\prime}\circ g^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}((f^{\prime})^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Gamma))_{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g^{\prime})^{\ast}((f^{\prime})^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{f^{\prime},g^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}(f^{\prime}\circ g^{\prime})}

This shows that
\chi
is an adjoint equivalence.

Consider now a chain of morphisms

\mathcal{W}\xrightarrow{h}\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{g}\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}

in
\mathpzc{K}
. The following equality of 2-natural transformation holds

\chi_{f,g\circ h}\circ(\chi_{g,h}\star[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f))=\chi_{f\circ g,h}\circ([{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](h)\star\chi_{f,g})

because it holds on components thanks to the compatibility between the iso-comma objects along the composite of three morphisms and the pasting of the three iso-comma objects. This shows that the invertible modification
\omega
required in Definition
2.7
is the identity for
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
.

Moreover, the invertible modification
\gamma
of Definition
2.7
, is the identity as well. Indeed, given a morphism
\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}
in
\mathpzc{K}
, the pseudonatural transformations
\chi_{f,\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}}\circ(\iota\star[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f))
and
\operatorname{id}_{[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)}
have the same components by the universal properties of the iso-comma object and the same structure 2-cells thanks to the fact that the structure 2-cells of
\iota
of the form
\iota_{[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\varphi)}
with
\varphi
a morphism in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
are identities by definition of
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)
. Analogously, the invertible modification
\delta
of Definition
2.7
is the identity as well.

Finally, the axioms of trihomomorphisms are trivially satisfied by
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
since all the modifications involved are identities.
∎

Analogously to the one-dimensional case, we can consider the particular case of
[\mathcal{T}/\mathcal{G}]
where
\mathcal{T}
is the terminal object of
\mathpzc{K}
and so the morphisms with target
\mathcal{T}
are uniquely determined and the 2-category
[{\mathcal{T}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
is isomorphic to
2\mathpzc{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{Y})
.

Definition 2.10
.

The quotient pre-2-stack
[{\mathcal{T}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
is called
classifying pre-2-stack
and will be denoted
2\mathcal{B}G
.

In the remaining part of this section we will prove that, if the
(2,1)
-category
\mathpzc{K}
is bicocomplete and such that iso-comma objects preserve bicolimits and the bitopology
\tau
is subcanonical, the quotient pre-2-stacks of Definition
2.1
are 2-stacks in the sense of
[
6
]
. To prove this important result, we will use the following explicit characterization of 2-stack that we prove in
[
6
]
.

Theorem 2.11

(
[
6
]
, Theorem 3.18 )
.

Let
(\mathpzc{K},\tau)
be a bisite. A trihomomorphism
F\colon\mathpzc{K}^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathpzc{Bicat}
is a 2-stack if and only if for every
C\in\mathpzc{K}
and every covering bisieve
S\in\tau(C)
the following conditions are satisfied:

(O)

every weak descent datum for
S
of elements of
F
is weakly effective;

(M)

every descent datum for
S
of morphisms of
F
is effective;

(2C)

every matching family for
S
of 2-cells of
F
has a unique amalgamation.

Moreover, we will need the crucial result that every object of a subcanonical bisite is the sigma-bicolimit of each covering bisieve over it. We prove this result in
[
6
]
.

Theorem 2.12

(
[
6
]
,Theorem 2.24)
.

Let
\tau
be a subcanonical bitopology and let
S\colon R\Rightarrow y(C)
be a covering bisieve over
C
. Then
S
is a sigma-bicolim bisieve, that is

C=\sigma\mbox{-}\hskip 0.64586pt{\bbicolim}\hskip 0.43057pt{F}

where
F\colon\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}\to\mathpzc{K}
is the 2-functor of projection to the first component.

Another important result proven in
[
6
]
that will be helpful for the proof is the following.

Proposition 2.13

(
[
6
]
,Proposition 2.26)
.

Let
\mathpzc{K}
be a small 2-category with iso-comma objects and let
\tau
be a subcanonical bitopology on it. Let then
S\colon R\Rightarrow\mathpzc{K}(-,Y)
be a covering bisieve over
Y\in\mathpzc{K}
and
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism in
\mathpzc{K}
. Then
X
is the sigma-bicolimit of the 2-functor

F\colon{\scalebox{1.2}{$\int$}}\hskip-0.6458ptR\xrightarrow{\operatorname{inc}}{\raisebox{1.02228pt}{$\mathpzc{K}$}\mkern-1.0mu\left/{}_{\mkern-4.1mu\operatorname{lax}}\right.\raisebox{-2.55554pt}{$Y$}}\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}}{\raisebox{1.02228pt}{$\mathpzc{K}$}\mkern-1.0mu\left/{}_{\mkern-4.1mu\operatorname{lax}}\right.\raisebox{-2.55554pt}{$X$}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{dom}}\mathpzc{K}

where
\operatorname{inc}=\int{\hskip-2.15277ptS}\colon\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}\to\int{\hskip-2.15277pt\mathpzc{K}(-,Y)}
is the inclusion 2-functor and
f^{\ast}
is the 2-functor of iso-comma object along the morphism
f
.

Finally, we will need to apply the results about colimits in lax slices proved by Mesiti in
[
13
]
and more in detail in the case of sigma-bicolimits in his PhD thesis
[
14
]
.

Theorem 2.14
.

Let
\mathpzc{K}
be a bicocomplete
(2,1)
-category with finite flexible limits and such that iso-comma objects preserve bicolimits. Let then
\tau
be a subcanonical bitopology on
\mathpzc{K}
and
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{G}\in\mathpzc{K}
with
\mathcal{G}
an internal 2-group. Then the quotient pre-2-stack
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
is a 2-stack.

Remark 2.15
.

The hypothesis that iso-comma objects preserve bicolimits in
\mathpzc{K}
means that for every morphism
a\colon\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{W}
in
\mathpzc{K}
the 2-functor

a^{\ast}\colon{\raisebox{1.00006pt}{$\mathpzc{K}$}\mkern-1.0mu\left/{}_{\mkern-4.1mu\operatorname{lax}}\right.\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\mathcal{W}$}}\to{\raisebox{1.00006pt}{$\mathpzc{K}$}\mkern-1.0mu\left/{}_{\mkern-4.1mu\operatorname{lax}}\right.\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\mathcal{T}$}}

of iso-comma along the morphism
a
preserves bicolimits. This hypothesis will be crucial in our proof of Theorem
2.14
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{Y}\in\mathpzc{K}
and let
S\colon R\Rightarrow\mathpzc{K}(-,\mathcal{Y})
be a covering bisieve over
\mathcal{Y}
. Thanks to Theorem
2.11
, it suffices to prove that the following conditions hold:

(O)

every weak descent datum for
S
of elements of
F
is weakly effective;

(M)

every descent datum for
S
of morphisms of
F
is effective;

(2C)

every matching family for
S
of 2-cells of
F
has a unique amalgamation.

We start proving (2C).

Let
\sigma,\rho\colon(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}})\to(\mathcal{Q},\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}})
be morphisms in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
. Consider a matching family for
S
of 2-cells of
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
that assigns to each morphism
f\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}\in S
a 2-cell

w_{f}\colon[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\sigma)\Rightarrow[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\rho)

We need to prove that there exists a unique 2-cell

w\colon\sigma\Rightarrow\rho

such that
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(w)=w_{f}
for every
f\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}\in S
.

By Proposition
2.13
,
\mathcal{P}
is the sigma-bicolimit of the 2-functor

F\colon{\scalebox{1.2}{$\int$}}\hskip-0.6458ptR\xrightarrow{\operatorname{inc}}{\raisebox{1.00006pt}{$\mathpzc{K}$}\mkern-1.0mu\left/{}_{\mkern-4.1mu\operatorname{lax}}\right.\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\mathcal{Y}$}}\xrightarrow{\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}}{\raisebox{1.00006pt}{$\mathpzc{K}$}\mkern-1.0mu\left/{}_{\mkern-4.1mu\operatorname{lax}}\right.\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\mathcal{P}$}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{dom}}\mathpzc{K}

with universal sigma-bicocone given by the identical 2-cells of the form

(6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{h}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\widetilde{l\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F((\operatorname{id},\alpha))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}(\widetilde{l\circ g})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F((g,\operatorname{id}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}l}

for every morphism
(g,\alpha)\colon(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}\xrightarrow{h}\mathcal{Y})\to(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{l}\mathcal{Y})
in
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
.

For every
f\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}\in S
, we consider the 2-cell

(7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\sigma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\rho)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

Using the fact that the
w_{f}
’s are the assignment of a matching family, it is straightforward to prove that the 2-cells of the form (
7
) are compatible. In particular, one needs to use the compatibility of the matching family with respect to composition to deal with the morphisms of
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
of type
(g,\operatorname{id})
and the compatibility of the matching family with respect to the 2-cells in
S
to deal with the morphisms of
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
of type
(\operatorname{id},\alpha)
.
So, by the two-dimensional universal property of the sigma-bicolimit of
F
, there exists a unique 2-cell
w\colon\sigma\Rightarrow\rho
such that for every
f\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}\in S
, the whiskering

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}

is equal to the 2-cell of diagram (
7
). We now show that
w
is the desired amalgamation. By definition of
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
, the 2-cell
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(w)
is the unique that has whiskering with
f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}
equal to the identical 2-cell and whiskering with
\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f
equal to the 2-cell

(8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\sigma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\rho)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}}

But the whiskering of
w_{f}
with
f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}
is equal to the identical 2-cell because
w_{f}
is a 2-cell in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Z})
and the 2-cells
\gamma_{[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\sigma)}
and
\gamma_{[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(\rho)}
are identities by definition of
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
. Moreover, the whiskering of
w_{f}
with
\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f
, is equal to the 2-cell of diagram (
8
) by construction of
w
. This shows that
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(w)=w_{f}
and concludes the proof of the condition (2C).

We now prove the condition
(M)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\alpha_{\mathcal{P}}),(\mathcal{Q},\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}})\in[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
. Consider a descent datum for
S
of morphisms of
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
that assigns to every
\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}\in S
a morphism

w_{f}\colon\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt{\mathcal{Z}}

and to every 2-cell
\Gamma\colon f\Rightarrow f^{\prime}
with
f,f^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}\in S
an (invertible) 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}{}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Gamma)_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Gamma)_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f^{\prime}}}

Moreover, for every chain
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}
with
f\in S
, we have an (invertible) 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f\circ g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f\circ g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}w_{f}}
\sim
\sim
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,g}}
\simeq
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f\circ g}}

We need to prove that there exist a morphism

w\colon\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{Q}

in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
and for every morphism
\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}\in S
an (invertible) 2-cell

\psi^{f}\colon w_{f}\Rightarrow[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](f)(w)

that satisfy the conditions required by the definition of effective descent datum on morphisms (see Definition 3.9 of
[
6
]
).
The idea is to induce the morphism
w
using the universal property of
\mathcal{P}
seen as a sigma-bicolimit of a diagram of shape
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
. This is similar to what we did to prove the condition (2C).

As already observed when proving (2C), by Proposition
2.13
,
\mathcal{P}
is the sigma-bicolimit of the 2-functor

F\colon{\scalebox{1.2}{$\int$}}\hskip-0.6458ptR\xrightarrow{\operatorname{inc}}{\raisebox{1.00006pt}{$\mathpzc{K}$}\mkern-1.0mu\left/{}_{\mkern-4.1mu\operatorname{lax}}\right.\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\mathcal{Y}$}}\xrightarrow{\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}}{\raisebox{1.00006pt}{$\mathpzc{K}$}\mkern-1.0mu\left/{}_{\mkern-4.1mu\operatorname{lax}}\right.\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\mathcal{P}$}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{dom}}\mathpzc{K}

with universal sigma-bicocone as shown in diagram (
6
).

We construct a sigma-bicocone
\Xi
of shape
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
over
\mathcal{Q}
. Given an object
(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y})
, we define
\Xi_{f}
as the composite

\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{w_{f}}\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}}\mathcal{Q}

Given a morphism
(g,\operatorname{id})\colon(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\widetilde{f\circ g})\to(\mathcal{Z},f)
in
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, we define
\Xi_{(g,\operatorname{id})}
as the following 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F((g,\operatorname{id}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{g}_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{f\circ g})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f}

where
\widehat{g}_{\mathcal{Q}}
is the morphism induced by the universal property of
{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}
using the 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{f\circ g})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widetilde{f\circ g})^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f\circ g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{f,g}}

and the 2-cell
\kappa_{g}
is induced by the two-dimensional universal property of
{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}
using the compatible 2-cells

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{{f}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{(\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{{f}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F((g,\operatorname{id}))}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}}f}
\sim
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\varphi^{f,g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}w_{f}}
\sim
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{(\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{{f}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{(\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{{f}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z})\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F((g,\operatorname{id}))}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{g}_{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{w_{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}}
\sim
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\varphi^{f,g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}w_{f}}
\sim
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{g}_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f}

where the unnamed arrows are the projections and the 2-cell
\widehat{\varphi^{f,g}}
is defined by whiskering
\varphi^{f,g}
with the isomorphic 2-cells of pseudoinvertibility of the involved equivalences.

Given a morphism
(\operatorname{id},\Gamma)\colon(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y})\to(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}}\mathcal{Y})
in
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, we define
\Xi_{(\operatorname{id},\Gamma)}
as the following 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F((\operatorname{id},\Gamma))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\Gamma)_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f^{\prime}}

One can then construct the structure 2-cell of the form
\Xi_{(g,\alpha)}
using the factorization of
(g,\alpha)
as a composite of a morphism of type
(g,\operatorname{id})
with one of type
(\operatorname{id},\alpha)
and use the compatibility conditions satisfied by the descent datum to verify that these assignment defines a sigma-bicocone of shape
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
over
\mathcal{Q}
. This implies that there exists a unique morphism
w\colon\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{Q}
together (invertible) 2-cells

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{f}}

\simeq

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f}

for every
\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}
such that, given a morphism
(g,\alpha)\colon(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y})\to(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{h}\mathcal{Y})
, the following equality of 2-cells holds:

(9)
