We now need to prove that
w\colon\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{Q}
is the desired morphism. It is straightforward to prove that
w
is a morphism in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
. Moreover, given
\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}\in S
, we can induce the desired 2-cell
\psi^{f}\colon w_{f}\Rightarrow f^{\ast}w
applying the two-dimensional universal property of the iso-comma object
\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}
to the compatible 2-cells

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\theta}_{f}}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{Q}}f}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{f}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{w_{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}}

Finally, one can prove that the required conditions are satisfied tanks to equalities of type (
9
) and the compatibility conditions satisfied by the descent datum. This concludes the proof that (M) holds.

It remains to prove that the condition (O) holds. This will be the trickiest condition to prove.

Consider a weak descent datum for S of elements of
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}]
that assigns to every
\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}\in S
a principal
\mathcal{G}
-2-bundle

\pi_{f}\colon\mathcal{W}_{f}\to\mathcal{Z}\in[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Z})

and to every 2-cell
\Gamma\colon f\Rightarrow f^{\prime}
with
f,f^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
a morphism

w_{\Gamma}\colon\mathcal{W}_{f}\Rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}

in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Z})
. Moreover, for every chain
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}
with
f\in S
, we have an equivalence

\varphi^{f,g}\colon\mathcal{W}_{f\circ g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.01389pt}{\kern-0.90417pt$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}{{\rightarrow}}g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}

in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Z})
and all the other data required by the definition of weak descent datum (see
[
6
]
) satisfying the required axioms.

We need to prove that there exist a principal
\mathcal{G}
-2-bundle

\pi\colon\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{Y}\in[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})

and for every
\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}\in S
an equivalence

\psi^{f}\colon W_{f}\to f^{\ast}W

in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
and all the other data required by the definition of weak descent datum satisfying the required conditions.

We use the assignment of the weak descent datum to construct a pseudofunctor

\Lambda\colon\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}\to{\raisebox{1.00006pt}{$\mathpzc{K}$}\mkern-1.0mu\left/{}_{\mkern-4.1mu\operatorname{ps}}\right.\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\mathcal{Y}$}}

Given an object
(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y})\in\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, we define
\Lambda((\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}))
as the morphism

\mathcal{W}_{f}\xrightarrow{\pi_{f}}\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}

Given a morphism
(g,\operatorname{id})\colon(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\widetilde{f\circ g})\to(\mathcal{Z},f)
in
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, we define
\Lambda((g,\operatorname{id}))
as the 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\varphi^{f,g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\pi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{\pi_{f},g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f\circ g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{-1}_{f,g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Given a morphism
(\operatorname{id},\Gamma)\colon(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y})\to(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}}\mathcal{Y})
in
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, we define
\Lambda((\operatorname{id},\Gamma))
as the 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{f^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{-1}}

One can then construct the image of a generic morphism of
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
using the factorization in terms of morphisms of type
(g,\operatorname{id})
and
(\operatorname{id},\Gamma)
.
Finally, given a 2-cell
\delta\colon(g,\alpha)\to(h,\beta)
in
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
with
(g,\alpha),(h,\beta)\colon(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y})\to(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}}\mathcal{Y})
, we define
\Lambda(\delta)
as the 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f^{\prime}\circ g}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f^{\prime}\circ h}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{R(\delta)_{f^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f^{\prime},g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f^{\prime}}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\delta)_{\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\alpha_{\delta})_{f^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f^{\prime},g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f^{\prime}}h}

where
(\alpha_{\delta})_{f^{\prime}}
is the 2-cell assigned by the weak descent datum (see Definition 3.14 of
[
6
]
).

We now prove that
\Lambda
is a pseudofunctor.

Given a chain of morphisms

(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y})\xrightarrow{(g,\alpha)}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}}\mathcal{Y})\xrightarrow{(g^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{Y})

in
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, the isomorphic 2-cell

\varepsilon_{(g,\alpha),(g^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})}\colon\Lambda((g^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}))\circ\Lambda((g,\alpha))\Rightarrow\Lambda((g^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})\circ(g,\alpha))

is given by the following pasting diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f^{\prime}\circ g}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f^{\prime\prime}\circ g^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g^{\prime})^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}\circ g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{\widetilde{(f^{\prime\prime}\circ g^{\prime})}\circ g}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f^{\prime\prime}\circ g^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}(g^{\prime})^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f^{\prime\prime}\circ(g^{\prime}\circ g)}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g^{\prime}\circ g)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\alpha^{\prime}\star g}}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f^{\prime},g}}

\simeq

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f^{\prime}}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f^{\prime\prime},g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f^{\prime\prime}}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\zeta}}

\simeq

\simeq
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f^{\prime\prime}\circ g^{\prime},g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\varphi^{f^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime}}}
\sim
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{g^{\prime},g}_{f^{\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime}\circ g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f^{\prime\prime}}(g^{\prime}\circ g)}

where
\beta^{g^{\prime},g}_{f^{\prime\prime}}
is the weak cocycle condition on
f^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime},g
and
\zeta
is the 2-cell such that
(g,\alpha)\circ(g^{\prime},\alpha)=(g\circ g^{\prime},\zeta)
.
Moreover, it is straightforward to prove that
\Lambda((\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id}))
is isomorphic to the identity and that the required conditions on 2-cells are satisfied.

Let then
W
be the sigma-bicolimit of the pseudofunctor
\operatorname{dom}\circ\Lambda
(that exists because
\mathpzc{K}
has all sigma-bicolimits by hypothesis). Since
\Lambda
is pseudofunctorial it corresponds to a sigma-bicocone over
\mathcal{Y}
of shape
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
and so, using the universal property of the sigma-bicolimit, we can induce a morphism

\pi\colon\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{Y}

Moreover, by the calculus of colimits in lax slices developed by Mesiti in
[
13
]
(and more in detail in
[
14
]
in the sigma-bicolimit case), we obtain the following equality

\pi=\sigma\mbox{-}\hskip 0.64586pt{\bbicolim}\hskip 0.43057pt{\Lambda}

We now prove that the morphism
\pi
is a principal
\mathcal{G}
-2-bundle.
\pi
is trivially
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant (since we are considering trivial actions of
\mathcal{G}
) so it remains to prove that it is 2-locally trivial.
Given
\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}\in S
, since
\pi_{f}\colon\mathcal{W}_{f}\to\mathcal{Z}
is 2-locally trivial, there exists a covering bisieve
S_{f}\in\tau(\mathcal{Z})
such that for every
\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{h}\mathcal{Z}\in S_{f}
the iso-comma object of
\pi_{f}
and
h
is equivalent to the product
\mathcal{G}\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}
. Consider then the collection

S^{\prime}=\{\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{h}\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}|f\in S,h\in S_{f}\}

Using the axiom
(T2)
of bitopology, it is easy to prove that
S^{\prime}
is a covering bisieve over
\mathcal{Y}
. Given a morphism
\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{l}\mathcal{Y}\in S^{\prime}
that is equal to the composite
\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{r}\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}\mathcal{Y}
, we obtain the following chain of equalities

l^{\ast}\pi=l^{\ast}\sigma\mbox{-}\hskip 0.64586pt{\bbicolim}\hskip 0.43057pt{\Lambda}=\sigma\mbox{-}\hskip 0.64586pt{\bbicolim}\hskip 0.43057pt{l^{\ast}\Lambda},

where the second equality is given by the hypothesis that iso-comma objects preserve colimits.
And given an object
(\mathcal{Z},f)\in\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
the following is an iso-comma object square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip-0.43057pt(\mathcal{Z}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{Z}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime})\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}\hskip-0.43057pt\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Y}}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\ast}g)^{\ast}\pi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{f}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\ast}g)^{\ast}r}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

thanks to the fact that the covering bisieve generated by
(f^{\ast}g)^{\ast}h
with
h\in S_{g}
trivializes
(f^{\ast}g)^{\ast}\pi_{f}
as shown in the proof of Proposition
1.19
. This implies that the iso-comma of the morphism
\mathcal{W}_{f}\xrightarrow{\pi_{f}}\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}
along
l
is equivalent to the product
\mathcal{G}\hskip-0.43057pt\times\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{T}
. And so the morphism
\pi
is 2-locally trivial.

Furthermore, we can induce a morphism
\alpha\colon\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{X}
applying the universal property of the sigma-bicolimit to the sigma-bicocone of shape
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
whose structure 2-cells are simply given by the fact that the morphisms involved are morphisms in 2-categories of the form
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{T})
with
\mathcal{T}\in\mathpzc{K}
. This conclude the proof of the fact that
(\mathcal{W}\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{W}\xrightarrow{\alpha}\mathcal{X})
is in
[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\mathcal{Y})
.

We now need to induce the equivalence
\psi^{f}\colon\mathcal{W}_{f}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.01389pt}{\kern-0.90417pt$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}{{\rightarrow}}f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}
for every
f\in S
. We equivalently induce the pseudoinverse
\theta^{f}\colon f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{W}_{f}
.
We observe that the following chain of equalities holds

f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}=f^{\ast}\sigma\mbox{-}\hskip 0.64586pt{\bbicolim}\hskip 0.43057pt{\Lambda}=\sigma\mbox{-}\hskip 0.64586pt{\bbicolim}\hskip 0.43057pt{f^{\ast}\Lambda}

and so we can use the universal property of the sigma-bicolimit to induce
\theta^{f}
. To do so, we construct a sigma bicocone
\Sigma
over
\mathcal{W}_{f}
. Given
(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}\mathcal{Y})\in\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, we define
\Sigma_{g}
as the composite

f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}\xrightarrow{\lambda_{f,g}}(g^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}\xrightarrow{\rho^{g,g^{\ast}f}}\mathcal{W}_{g\circ g^{\ast}f}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{W}_{\sigma_{f,g}}}\mathcal{W}_{f\circ f^{\ast}g}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}g}}(f^{\ast}g)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}\xrightarrow{\pi^{\ast}_{f}(f^{\ast}g)}\mathcal{W}_{f},

where
\lambda_{f,g}
is the isomorphism induced by the universal property of
(g^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}
using the 2-cell of iso-comma object of
f
and
g\circ\pi_{g}
and
\rho^{g,g^{\ast}f}
is the pseudoinverse of
\psi^{g,g^{\ast}f}
.

Given a morphism
(\operatorname{id},\alpha)\colon(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},g)\to(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},g^{\prime})
in
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, the structure 2-cell
\Sigma_{(\operatorname{id},\alpha)}
is given by the pasting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{g\circ g^{\ast}f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f\circ f^{\ast}g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}g)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((g^{\prime})^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{g^{\prime}\circ(g^{\prime})^{\ast}f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f\circ f^{\ast}g^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}g^{\prime})^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f,g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{g,g^{\ast}f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\sigma_{f,g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f}(f^{\ast}g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f}(f^{\ast}\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta}

\simeq

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f,g^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{g^{\prime},(g^{\prime})^{\ast}f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\sigma_{f,g^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}g^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f}(f^{\ast}g^{\prime})}

where the morphism
\delta_{\alpha}
is induced by the universal property of the iso-comma object
((g^{\prime})^{\ast}f)^{\ast}W_{g^{\prime}}
using the 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{g^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}\hskip-0.43057pt{\times}^{g^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hskip-0.43057pt\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{\pi_{g},g^{\ast}f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{g^{\prime}}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}f}

and the 2-cell
\zeta
is given by the following pasting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{g\circ g^{\ast}f}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f\circ f^{\ast}g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}g)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((g^{\prime})^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{g^{\prime}\circ(g^{\ast}f\circ f^{\ast}\alpha)}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{\widetilde{f\circ f^{\ast}g^{\prime}}A\circ f^{\ast}\alpha}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}\alpha)^{\ast}(f^{\ast}g^{\prime})^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{\widetilde{(g^{\prime}\circ(g^{\prime})^{\ast}f)}\circ f^{\ast}\alpha}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}\alpha)^{\ast}((g^{\prime})^{\ast}f)\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}\alpha)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{g\circ(g^{\prime})^{\ast}f}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}\alpha)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ(f^{\prime})^{\ast}g}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((g^{\prime})^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{(g^{\prime})\circ(g^{\prime})^{\ast}f}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f\circ f^{\ast}g^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}g^{\prime})^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{g,g^{\ast}f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\sigma_{f,g}}}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f}(f^{\ast}\alpha)}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{g^{\prime},(g^{\prime})^{\ast}f\circ f^{\ast}\alpha}}
\simeq
\cong
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f\circ f^{\ast}g,f^{\ast}\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{f^{\ast}g^{\prime},f^{\ast}\alpha}_{f}}
\cong
\sim
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{g^{\prime}\circ(g^{\prime})^{\ast}f,f^{\ast}\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{(g^{\prime})^{\ast}f,f^{\ast}\alpha}_{g^{\prime}}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\ast}\alpha)^{\ast}\rho^{g^{\prime},(g^{\prime})^{\ast}f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\ast}\alpha)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\sigma_{f,g^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{g^{\prime}\circ(g^{\prime})^{\ast}f,f^{\ast}\alpha}}
\cong
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\ast}\alpha)^{\ast}\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}g^{\prime}}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{g^{\prime},{g^{\prime}}^{\ast}f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\sigma_{f,g^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}g^{\prime}}}

Given a morphism
(h,\operatorname{id})\colon(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime\prime},\widetilde{g\circ h})\to(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},g)
in
\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, the structure 2-cell
\Sigma_{(h,\operatorname{id})}
is given by the pasting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{g\circ h}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(({\widetilde{g\circ h}})^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{{\widetilde{g\circ h}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{({\widetilde{g\circ h}})\circ(({\widetilde{g\circ h}})^{\ast}f)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f\circ f^{\ast}({\widetilde{g\circ h}})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}({\widetilde{g\circ h}}))^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}h^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{g\circ g^{\ast}f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f\circ f^{\ast}g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}g)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\varphi^{g,h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f,{\widetilde{g\circ h}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{g,h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{{\widetilde{g\circ h}},({\widetilde{g\circ h}})^{\ast}f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\sigma_{f,{\widetilde{g\circ h}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}({\widetilde{g\circ h}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}

\simeq

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f}(f^{\ast}(\widetilde{g\circ h}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{g,h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\pi^{\ast}_{g}h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f,g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{g,g^{\ast}f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\sigma_{f,g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}_{f}(f^{\ast}g)}

where the morphism

is induced by the universal property of the iso-comma object

using the 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((\widetilde{g\circ h})^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{g\circ h}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{g\circ h}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{g\circ\pi_{g},f}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{g,h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\widetilde{g\circ h}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\ast}\pi_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{g\circ h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{-1}_{g,h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

the morphism
l_{g,h}
is induced by the universal property of the iso-comma object
(f^{\ast}g)^{\ast}W_{f}
using the 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}(\widetilde{g\circ h}))^{\ast}W_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{Z}^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{\pi_{f},f^{\ast}(\widetilde{g\circ h})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}(\widetilde{g\circ h})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\sigma_{g,h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}g}

the commutativity of the diagram on the left can be checked on components and the 2-cell
\xi
is obtained as a pasting of appropriate weak cocycle condition 2-cells and cells given by the assignment of the descent datum in a way similar to the one used to construct
\zeta
in
\Sigma_{(\operatorname{id},\alpha)}
.
One can then prove that these data give a sigma-bicocone
\Sigma
. And we can thus induce the desired morphism
\theta^{f}
. Furthermore, since the morphism

f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}\xrightarrow{\lambda_{f,g}}(g^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{g}\xrightarrow{\rho^{g,g^{\ast}f}}\mathcal{W}_{g\circ g^{\ast}f}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{W}_{\sigma_{f,g}}}\mathcal{W}_{f\circ f^{\ast}g}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}g}}(f^{\ast}g)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}

is an equivalence, it follows that
\theta^{f}
is an equivalence as well. And so we have induced the desired equivalence
\psi^{f}\colon\mathcal{W}_{f}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.01389pt}{\kern-0.90417pt$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}{{\rightarrow}}f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}
(pseudoinverse of
\theta^{f}
).

We now need to prove that, given
\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{Y}\in S
and a morphism
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}\mathcal{Z}
, there exists an invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\widetilde{f\circ g})^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f\circ g)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\psi^{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varepsilon_{g})_{f}}
\simeq
\sim

We equivalently prove that there exists an invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f\circ g)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\widetilde{f\circ g})^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\theta^{f}}
\sim
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{f,g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{g})_{f}}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}

We notice that

g^{\ast}f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}=g^{\ast}f^{\ast}\sigma\mbox{-}\hskip 0.64586pt{\bbicolim}\hskip 0.43057pt{(\operatorname{dom}\circ\Lambda)}=\sigma\mbox{-}\hskip 0.64586pt{\bbicolim}\hskip 0.43057pt{(g^{\ast}\circ f^{\ast}\circ\operatorname{dom}\circ\Lambda)}

and so we can induce the 2-cell
(\iota_{g})_{f}
via the universal property of the sigma-bicolimit. Given
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{t}\mathcal{Y})\in\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, we define a 2-cell
\Gamma_{t}
as the following pasting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}(t^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{t\circ t^{\ast}f}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ f^{\ast}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}(f^{\ast}t)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{m^{\ast}(t^{\ast}f)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{m^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{t\circ t^{\ast}f}}},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{m^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f\circ f^{\ast}t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{m^{\ast}(f^{\ast}t)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g^{\ast}f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{\widetilde{t\circ t^{\ast}f}\circ m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{{f\circ f^{\ast}t}\circ m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f\circ g)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f\circ g)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(t^{\ast}(f\circ g))^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{t\circ t^{\ast}(\widetilde{f\circ g})}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ f^{\ast}t\circ m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(f^{\ast}t\circ m)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ g\circ(f\circ g)^{\ast}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g\circ(f\circ g)^{\ast}t)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((f\circ g)^{\ast}t)^{\ast}(g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{\widetilde{f\circ g}\circ(f\circ g)^{\ast}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((f\circ g)^{\ast}t)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\widetilde{f\circ g})^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(t^{\ast}(\widetilde{f\circ g}))^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{t\circ t^{\ast}(\widetilde{f\circ g})}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{(\widetilde{f\circ g})\circ(\widetilde{f\circ g})^{\ast}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((\widetilde{f\circ g})^{\ast}t)^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{f\circ g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\widetilde{f\circ g})^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}}
\sim
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}f^{\ast}\sigma_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}f^{\ast}\sigma_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\lambda_{f,t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\theta^{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{f,g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\rho^{t,t^{\ast}f}}
\simeq
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{\sigma_{f,t}}}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}t}}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}(\pi^{\ast}_{f}(f^{\ast}t))}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{t^{\ast}f,m}_{t}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{\ast}\rho^{t,t^{\ast}f}}
\simeq
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\widetilde{t\circ t^{\ast}f},m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{f^{\ast}t,m}_{f}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{\ast}\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\widetilde{f\circ f^{\ast}t},m}}
\simeq
\simeq
\cong
\simeq
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\widetilde{f\circ f^{\ast}t},m}}
\cong
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f\circ g)^{\ast}\sigma_{t}}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f\circ g,t}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{t,t^{\ast}f\circ m}}
\cong
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,f^{\ast}t\circ m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{g,(f\circ g)^{\ast}t}_{f}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{f,g\circ(f\circ g)^{\ast}t}}
\simeq
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((f\circ g)^{\ast}t)^{\ast}\rho^{f,g}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\widetilde{f\circ g},(f\circ g)^{\ast}t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((f\circ g)^{\ast}t)^{\ast}\varphi^{f,g}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\widetilde{f\circ g},t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widetilde{f\circ g})^{\ast}\sigma_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{t,t^{\ast}(\widetilde{f\circ g})}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{f\circ g},t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\widetilde{f\circ g},(\widetilde{f\circ g})^{\ast}t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{\widetilde{f\circ g}}}

where
\varepsilon
is the canonical equivalence between
(f\circ g)^{\ast}\mathcal{T}
and
g^{\ast}f^{\ast}\mathcal{T}
and
m=(f^{\ast}t)^{\ast}g\circ\varepsilon
. Using the compatibility conditions of the weak descent datum, one can then prove that the
\Gamma_{t}
’s form a modification and so the 2-cell
(\iota_{g})_{f}
is induced by the universal property of the sigma-bicolimit.

We now need to prove that, given a 2-cell
\gamma\colon f\Rightarrow f^{\prime}
with
f,f^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
in
R(\mathcal{Z})
there exists an isomorphic 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{f^{\prime}}^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\gamma)_{\mathcal{W}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\gamma}}

\simeq

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{f^{\prime}}}

We equivalently induce an isomorphic 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{f^{\prime}}^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\gamma)_{\mathcal{W}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{\gamma}}

\simeq

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{f^{\prime}}}

We notice that

f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}=f^{\ast}\sigma\mbox{-}\hskip 0.64586pt{\bbicolim}\hskip 0.43057pt{\operatorname{dom}\circ\Lambda}=\sigma\mbox{-}\hskip 0.64586pt{\bbicolim}\hskip 0.43057pt{f^{\ast}\circ\operatorname{dom}\circ\Lambda}

and so we can induce the desired 2-cell
\kappa_{\gamma}
via the universal property of the sigma-bicolimit using a modification
\Delta
. Given
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{t}\mathcal{Y})\in\int{\hskip-2.15277ptR}
, we define the 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{f^{\prime}}^{\ast}\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{f^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\sigma_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}\sigma_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{t}}

\simeq

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[{\mathcal{X}}/{\mathcal{G}}](\gamma)_{\mathcal{W}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{f^{\prime}}}

as a pasting diagram analogous to the one used to define
\Gamma_{t}
. And it is easy to see that we obtain a modification
\Delta
that induces the desired 2-cell
\kappa_{\gamma}
.

Finally, it is straightforward to verify that the defined data satisfy the axioms required by the definition of weakly effective weak descent datum. This concludes the proof of the condition (O).
∎
